Let's design a more deterministic constructive approach based on solving the dual with iterative proportional fitting (IPF), which might converge to self-dual optimal.

Observation: The smooth fixed point p∝? If we have weights w over lags, the multiplicative update with G_i=2Σ w_k p_{k-i} drives p_i to decrease where G_i high. If w is kept such that weights proportional to exp(λ(b-M)), at a fixed point maybe G_i constant over support (since update no change): Σ w_k p_{k-i} constant for all i. This is the KKT condition with y=w. Additionally w_k ∝ exp(λ p*p_k - λM). Thus if p*p is flat at max on support of w and G constant, we have approximate optimal. This is like alternating:
1. Given p, set w = softmax(λ p*p) (active peaks).
2. Find p maximizing? Actually to make G constant, we need p be in the nullspace? The update p ← p exp(-η G) changes p until G constant. This is a system:
   p_i G_i = const (over positive support)
   w_k ∝ exp(λ b_k)
   G_i = 2Σ_k w_k p_{k-i}
   b_k =Σ_i p_i p_{k-i}
Can we solve this fixed point directly? This resembles finding eigenvectors of a linear operator depending on p. Maybe use multiplicative updates alternating p and w:
- Given w, the condition G_i constant can be achieved by p ∝ 1/G_0? But G depends linearly on p: G_i= (W_p)_i. Need p_i (W p)_i = C. This is nonlinear (quadratic). Algorithm:
- Start p.
- Loop:
  - Compute b, w = softmax(λ b) (or top band).
  - Compute G = 2 corr(w,p).
  - Update p = normalize(1/G) or p / G? If G_i>0. This equalizes G to uniform in one shot (since new p_i ∝1/oldG_i, then new G not necessarily constant). Multiplicative p*exp(-ηG) is small step; p = normalize(p / G) is large step to enforce G constant. Could be unstable but maybe good.

Detailed optimizer pseudocode:
```
def optimize_p(p, budget_s, rng, label):
    n=p.size
    L=np.fft.next_fast_len(2*n-1)
    p=p.copy(); p/=p.sum()
    best_local=p.copy(); best_score=score_fft(p)
    eta_scale = 0.2  # exponent avg -2*eta_scale
    beta_c = 20.0
    sigma = 0.1
    no_improve=0
    while time<deadline:
       b, M, score = eval_fft(p,L,n)
       if score < best_score - 1e-8:
           best_score=score; no_improve=0; maybe exact
       else: no_improve+=1
       if no_improve > 50:
            # perturb: block or exponent p *= exp(noise), beta/eta random, maybe restart from best_local with noise
       # weights
       Mmax=max(b,1e-300)
       beta = beta_c / Mmax
       z = beta*(b - Mmax)
       w = np.exp(clipped z); w.sum==0 fallback
       w = (1-floor_weight)*w + floor_weight*(1/(2n-1))
       # compute G
       Pft=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
       Wft=np.fft.rfft(w,L)
       corr=np.fft.irfft(Pft * np.conjugate(Wft), L)
       G=2.0*corr[:n]
       G = np.maximum(G, 1e-300)
       if G.sum()>0: G = 2.0*G/G.sum()
       # optionally smooth G with moving average to avoid overkill
       # smoothing kernel length ~3? Use np.convolve with ones/s.
       # update
       for scale in [eta_scale, 0.5*eta_scale, 0.25*eta_scale]:
            logfac = -scale*n*G  # since eta=scale*n
            if sigma>0: logfac += sigma*rng.normal(n)
            logfac -= np.max(logfac)
            p_try = p*np.exp(logfac)
            p_try = np.clip(p_try,0,None)
            p_try = p_try*(1-1e-7)+1e-7/n? maybe
            s=p_try.sum(); if s>0 p_try/=s
            score_try=score_fft(p_try)
            if score_try < best_score + 1e-6: p=p_try; break
       # if no accepted, perturb p (e.g., p *= exp(-eta_scale*n*G /2) regardless? or revert)
       # schedule reduce sigma
    return best_local
```
But computing Pft inside loop after b; we already had Pft to compute b. We can store. b computed with Pft. For each trial p_try, need new b; no stored.

```

   if time over break
   b,M,sc = eval p (if not cached)
   if sc < best_run_score - 1e-8:
       best_run_score=sc; best_run=p.copy(); last_improve=it
   if it - last_improve > patience:
       # perturbeta, beta, or reinitialize from best_run with random block

       p = perturb(best_run? or current?)
   compute weights,G
   # propose step
   eta_try = eta * rng.uniform(0.7,1.3)
   p_try = multiplicative(p, G, eta_try, sigma)
   sc_try = eval p_try
   if sc_try < sc + tol and sc_try < best_run_score + tol2? Accept if improves current or best?
   Accept criterion: if sc_try <= sc * (1+1e-6) (not much worse) to allow exploration; but if sc worse than best, may wander.    If accept: p=p_try, if sc_try<best...; eta *= 1.05 maybe.

Potential numerical issues:
- `np.fft.next_fast_len` available in numpy. Use if L none.
- `irfft` length L returns real length L. For b, first 2n-1 may include tiny negative; clip.
- For p sum extremely small, normalize.
- `evaluate_sequence` requires Python list floats. We convert.
- The evaluator caps 1000; our p sum1 so max≤1 (unless numerical negative clip). Good.
- If hidden length? We'll return `best_selected`. We can choose best exact among candidates.

Let's design code:

```
import time, math

Helper:
```
def norm_nonneg(a):
    arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr),arr,0.0)
    arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s<=1e-12:
        arr=np.ones_like(arr)*1.0
        s=arr.size
    arr = arr/s  # normalized sum 1
    return arr
```
But if length 0? handle.

`fast_eval(p)`:
```
def fast_eval(p, L=None):
    n=p.size
    if n==0: return (np.inf, np.array([]), np.inf)
    if L is None: L=np.fft.next_fast_len(2*n-1)
    F=np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    b=np.fft.irfft(F*F, L)[:2*n-1]
    np.maximum(b,0,out=b)
    M=float(b.max()) if b.size else 0.0
    score=2.0*n*M  # since sum p=1? if not, need (sum p)^2
    s=float(np.sum(p))
    if s>0: score=2.0*n*M/(s*s)
    else: score=np.inf
    return score,b,M
```
Since we normalize p before, s=1 but include for safety. If p sum not 1, gradient formulas assume derivative with p not normalized? We can normalize always.

`compute_G(p,w,L)`:
```
Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,L)
C=np.fft.irfft(Fp*np.conjugate(Fw), L)
G=2.0*C[:p.size]
G=np.where(np.isfinite(G),G,0.0)
G=np.maximum(G,0.0)
s=G.sum()
if s>0: G*=2.0/s
return G
```
Need ensure no wrap: L≥2n-1. `next_fast_len(2n-1)` ok. But correlation positive shifts up to 2n-2; L exactly 2n-1 no wrap; if next_fast_len returns 2n-1 if fast (not power2? next_fast_len may return non-power prime? FFT efficient but circular same; L≥2n-1 so no wrap. Good.

`softmax_weights(b, beta_c, floor_w)`:
```
M=b.max()
if M<=0: return uniform
beta=beta_c / M
z=beta*(b-M)
# clip z min -700
z=np.clip(z,-700,0)
w=np.exp(z)
s=w.sum()
if s<=0: w=np.full_like(b,1)
else: w/=s
# floor
w = (1-floor_w)*w + floor_w/(b.size)
w /= w.sum()
return w
```
If beta_c high, many underflow to 0, floor ensures.

`propose_update(p,G,eta_scale,n,sigma,rng)`:
```
logfac = -eta_scale*n*G
if sigma>0: logfac = logfac + sigma*rng.standard_normal(n)
logfac -= np.max(logfac)
nf = (1.0-1e-8)*p + 1e-8/n ?
p_new = p * np.exp(logfac)
p_new = np.clip(p_new,0,None)
# add small floor
p_new = (1.0 - floor)*p_new + floor*(1.0/n)
 s=p_new.sum(); if s==0 fallback
 return p_new/s
```
But if p_i zero, multiplying by exp gives zero; floor introduces tiny. Use floor=1e-8 maybe.

`block_perturb(p, rng, strength=0.25)`:
```
q=p.copy(); n=len(q)
w=int(rng.integers(max(2,n//100), max(3,n//4)))
st=int(rng.integers(0,n-w))
factor=np.exp(rng.normal(0,strength,size=n-w? only block))
q[st:st+w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,strength))
# maybe sym? no
# normalize
if rng.random()<0.2: q = (1-0.5? no)
return norm_nonneg(q) # but norm_nonneg scale to sum1, no cap issues
```

`make_patterns(n, rng, base_small)`:
Return list.
- uniform: np.ones/n.
- linear: for slope in [0.5,1,2,0.2?]: p=np.exp(slope*t) normalized. Also decreasing? increasing maybe. Use `p=1+slope*t` nonnegative.
- quadratic: p=np.exp(a*t + b*t*t). e.g. [0,2,-1], [2,-3], [-2,3] ensure nonnegative. Simpler: `t=(i+0.5)/n`; patterns:
```
patterns.append(normalize(1 + 1.0*t))
patterns.append(normalize(1 + 3.0*t))
patterns.append(normalize(1 + 3.0*t + 2.0*(t-1)**2))
patterns.append(normalize(np.exp(1.5*t)))
patterns.append(normalize(np.sin(π*t)**2 shifted?))
```
- sine: `1 + amp*np.sin(2π c t + phase)` for amp 0.5,1 (clip nonneg), c=1,2,3, phase maybe -π/2 to break.
- small base upsample.
- random: `p=np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.5,n)); smooth; normalize`.
Maybe return ~8 for small, ~10 for large.
Need avoid duplicates if base_small same.

`upsample(base, n)`:
```
base=norm_nonneg(base)
m=base.size
if m==n return base
q=n//m; r=n%m
out=np.empty(n); idx=0
for j in range(m):
   L = q + (1 if j<r else 0)
   out[idx:idx+L] = base[j]/L
   idx += L
return out
```
If m=0 fallback uniform.

`lp_polish`? Maybe optional. Let's skip to avoid complexity. But maybe include a very simple dual iterative scaling:
Given p, w top band, solve p_i proportional to 1/G_i (damped). This is like large step. We can incorporate as an update mode: every K iterations, try `p2 = normalize(p / np.maximum(G, eps))` or blend with p. Evaluate, accept if better. This "one-shot equalization" may escape. Add in optimize.

`optimize_sequence(p, rng, time_limit, label, best_tracker?)`: Because no closures, best exact update outside. Return best p_local, best_score_local, maybe best_fft.
Parameters: `verbosity` maybe.

Pseudo:
```
def optimize_sequence(p, rng, time_limit, label):
    p=norm_nonneg(p).copy()
    n=p.size
    L=np.fft.next_fast_len(2*n-1)
    best_p=p.copy()
    sc,b,M=fast_eval(p,L)
    best_sc=sc
    result_exact=None? not here.
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_limit
    eta=0.12
    beta_c=12
    sigma=0.12
    floor_w=1e-3
    it=0
    no=0
    max_no=80
    while time<deadline:
       it+=1
       # occasional global tuning
       if it % 20 ==0: beta_c = min(50, beta_c*1.05); sigma*=0.98
       # weights/gradient from current p and b
       if b is None or not cache:
          sc,b,M=fast_eval(p,L)
          if sc < best_sc - 1e-10:
             best_sc=sc; best_p=p.copy(); no=0
          else: no+=1
       else: no++? We'll compute each loop.
       if no > max_no:
          # perturb current from best with random block, randomize params
          p_try=block_perturb(best_p,rng,strength=np.sqrt(no/100)+0.3)
          sc_try,_,_=fast_eval(p_try,L)
          if sc_try < best_sc + 0.05:
              p=p_try
          else:
              p=block_perturb(p,rng,0.5)
          eta = rng.uniform(0.05,0.2)
          beta_c = rng.uniform(8,40)
          sigma = rng.uniform(0.05,0.25)
          no=0
          continue
       w=make_weights(b,beta_c,floor_w)
       G=compute_G(p,w,L)
       # one-shot equalization try occasionally
       if it % 50 ==0:
           G2 = np.maximum(G,1e-12)
           p2 = p / G2  # maybe
           p2 = norm_nonneg(p2)
           sc2,_,_=fast_eval(p2,L)
           if sc2 < best_sc:
               p=p2; best_p=p.copy(); best_sc=sc2
       # line search steps
       accepted=False
       for factor in [1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1]:
           p_try = propose_update(p,G,eta*factor,n,sigma,rng)
           sc_try,b_try,M_try=fast_eval(p_try,L)
           if sc_try < sc or (sc_try < best_sc + 0.02 and rng.random()<0.05):
               # accept
               p=p_try; b=b_try; M=M_try; sc=sc_try
               if sc < best_sc:
                   best_sc=sc; best_p=p.copy(); no=0
               eta=min(0.3? maybe)
               accepted=True
               break
       if not accepted:
           # try without noise with smaller eta?
           p_try=propose_update(p,G,eta*0.1,n,0.0,rng)
           sc_try,...
           if sc_try < sc:
                p=p_try;...
           else:
                eta *=0.7
           no += 3? 
       # if current score > best_sc + 0.05 and many rejects, reset p=best_p perturbed
    return best_p,best_sc
```

Let's detail `optimize_sequence` code:
```
def optimize_sequence(p, rng, time_limit):
    p = norm_nonneg(p).copy()
    n = p.size
    if n <= 1: return p, fast_eval(p)[0]
    L = np.fft.next_fast_len(2*n - 1)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(time_limit))
    cur_p = p
    cur_score, cur_b, cur_M = fast_eval(cur_p, L)
    best_p = cur_p.copy()
    best_score = cur_score
    eta = 0.12
    beta_c = 12.0
    sigma = 0.10
    floor_w = 1e-3
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1
        # sine/param schedule
        if it % 50 == 0:
            beta_c = min(60.0, beta_c * 1.06)
            sigma = max(0.02, sigma * 0.97)
        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-10:
            best_score = cur_score
            best_p = cur_p.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
        # stagnation handling
        if no_improve > 80:
            # perturb best
            for attempt in range(4):
                cand = block_perturb(best_p, rng, strength=0.25 + 0.1*attempt)
                sc2,b2,M2 = fast_eval(cand,L)
                if sc2 < best_score + 0.05:
                    cur_p=cand; cur_score=sc2; cur_b=b2; break
            else:
                cand = random_pattern(n, rng)
                cur_p=cand; cur_score,cur_b,cur_M=fast_eval(cur_p,L)
            eta = float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.25))
            beta_c = float(rng.uniform(8,50))
            sigma = float(rng.uniform(0.02,0.25))
            no_improve = 0
            continue
        # weights and gradient
        w = make_weights(cur_b, beta_c, floor_w, rng)
        G = compute_G(cur_p, w, L)
        # occasional equalization
        if it % 80 == 0:
            Gsafe = np.maximum(G, 1e-12)
            eq = cur_p / Gsafe
            eq = norm_nonneg(eq)
            sc_eq,b_eq,M_eq = fast_eval(eq,L)
            if sc_eq < best_score:
                cur_p=eq; cur_score=sc_eq; cur_b=b_eq; cur_M=M_eq
                best_score=sc_eq; best_p=cur_p.copy(); no_improve=0
                continue
        # base step with maybe line search if no_improve high
        factors = [1.0]
        if no_improve > 20: factors = [1.0,0.5,0.25]
        if no_improve > 60: factors += [0.1]
        accepted=False
        for f in factors:
            p_try = multiplicative_update(cur_p, G, eta*f, cur_score? n, sigma, rng)
            sc_try,b_try,M_try = fast_eval(p_try,L)
            if (sc_try < cur_score) or (sc_try < best_score + 0.02 and rng.random() < 0.03):
                cur_p=p_try; cur_score=sc_try; cur_b=b_try; cur_M=M_try
                accepted=True
                break
        if accepted:
            # if improved best, maybe increase eta slightly
            if cur_score < best_score:
                best_score=cur_score; best_p=cur_p.copy(); no_improve=0
                eta = min(0.35, eta*1.05)
            else:
                no_improve += 1
        else:
            # try a conservative deterministic step (no noise)
            p_try = multiplicative_update(cur_p, G, eta*0.1, n, 0.0, rng)
            sc_try,b_try,M_try = fast_eval(p_try,L)
            if sc_try < cur_score:
                cur_p=p_try; cur_score=sc_try; cur_b=b_try; cur_M=M_try
                if cur_score < best_score:
                    best_score=cur_score; best_p=cur_p.copy(); no_improve=0
            else:
                eta *= 0.75
                no_improve += 5
    return best_p, best_score
```

Potential issue: `multiplicative_update` with p zeros and floor. Use:
```
def multiplicative_update(p,G,eta,n,sigma,rng):
    logfac = -eta*n*G  # eta is scale
    if sigma>0:
       logfac = logfac + sigma*rng.standard_normal(n)
    logfac = logfac - np.max(logfac)
    p_new = p * np.exp(logfac)
    p_new = np.where(np.isfinite(p_new), p_new, 0.0)
    p_new = np.clip(p_new,0,1000)
    # add tiny uniform to recover zeros
    p_new = (1.0 - 1e-8)*p_new + 1e-8*(1.0/n)
    s=p_new.sum()
    if s<=0: return np.full(n,1.0/n)
    return p_new/s
```
If perturbation very large (eta*n*G large), all underflow? logfac centered by max, max maybe 0, so at least one coord factor1, others tiny. Good.

`make_weights` with floor: if beta_c/M overflows? M may be 1e-6; beta 12e6; z=beta*(b-M) negative large for all but max. `np.exp` of negative clipped -700 ok. `M` could be 0 if p zero; not.
Potential issue: b from FFT may have small negative clipped; max maybe slightly > true, fine.

But if input base from height_sequence_1 values maybe large up to 1000; normalize preserves relative, max after norm <=1 if nonnegative sum>0? Let's implement pattern helpers:
```
def linear_pattern(n,slope=1.0, curve=0.0):
   t=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/float(n)
   p=np.exp(slope*t + curve*t*t)
   return norm_nonneg(p)
def sine_pattern(n, amp=0.8, freq=1.0, phase=0.0, drift=0.0):
   t=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/float(n)
   p=1.0 + drift*t + amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*freq*t + phase)
   p=np.maximum(p,1e-6)
   return norm_nonneg(p)
```
Set sine with amp near1 can clip. Use `np.clip(p,1e-6,None)`.

`block_perturb`:
```
def block_perturb(p, rng, strength=0.3):
    q=p.copy(); n=q.size
    if n<=2: return q
    min_w=max(2,n//200); max_w=max(min_w+1,n//3)
    w=int(rng.integers(min_w, max_w))
    st=int(rng.integers(0,n-w))
    q[st:st+w] *= np.exp(float(rng.normal(0,strength)))
    # optionally scale another block inversely
    if n>=2*w:
       st2=int(rng.integers(0,n-w)); q[st2:st2+w] *= np.exp(float(rng.normal(0,strength/2)))
    return norm_nonneg(q)
```

def smooth_box(p,k):
    if k<2 or len(p)<3: return p
    k=min(k,len(p))
    ker=np.ones(k)/k
    q=np.convolve(p,ker,mode='same')
    return norm_nonneg(q)

```python
"""
Search for a nonnegative sequence minimizing
    2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / sum(a)^2.

The method is a hybrid randomized search:
- several short invariants are initialized from simple asymmetric shapes
  and from the provided starting construction if available;
- each sequence is optimized by a multiplicative/projected subgradient
  method on a smoothed max-convex objective;
- active convolution lags are emphasized with a softmax weight;
- periodic stagnation recovery uses block perturbations and occasional
  one-step inversions;
- the best exact-evaluated sequence found within the time budget is
  returned.

All returned coefficients are nonnegative and normalized to sum 1, which
keeps the evaluator's 1000 cap inactive and avoids the tiny-sum penalty.
"""

import time
import numpy as np


def norm_nonneg(a):
    """Coerce to a finite nonnegative float vector of sum 1."""
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.ndim != 1:
        arr = arr.reshape(-1)
    if arr.size == 0:
        arr = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        arr = np.ones_like(arr, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(arr.size)
    arr = arr / s
    return arr


def resize_sequence(seq, n):
    """Resize to length n by sum-preserving block expansion or subsampling."""
    seq = norm_nonneg(seq)
    m = seq.size
    n = int(max(1, n))
    if m == n:
        return seq.copy()
    if m < n:
        q, r = divmod(n, m)
        out = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
        pos = 0
        for j in range(m):
            L = q + (1 if j < r else 0)
            if L <= 0:
                continue
            out[pos:pos + L] = seq[j] / float(L)
            pos += L
        if pos < n:
            out[pos:] = 0.0
        return norm_nonneg(out)

    # m > n: simple evenly spaced subsampling followed by renormalization.
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    idx = np.linspace(0, m - 1, n)
    idx = np.round(idx).astype(int)
    idx = np.clip(idx, 0, m - 1)
    out = seq[idx].astype(np.float64, copy=True)
    # Smooth subsampling a little to avoid checkerboard artifacts.
    if n >= 3:
        k = min(5, n)
        ker = np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64) / float(k)
        out = np.convolve(out, ker, mode="same")
    return norm_nonneg(out)


def fast_eval(p, L=None):
    """
    Fast autocorrelation score using FFT.

    Returns (score, b, max_b).  score is exactly the evaluator objective
    for the current p, computed without np.convolve.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return (np.inf, np.array([], dtype=np.float64), 0.0)
    if L is None:
        L = int(np.fft.next_fast_len(2 * n - 1))
    F = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L, n=L)[:2 * n - 1]
    np.maximum(b, 0.0, out=b)
    M = float(b.max()) if b.size else 0.0
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return (np.inf, b, M)
    score = 2.0 * n * M / (s * s)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        score = np.inf
    return (score, b, M)


def make_weights(b, beta_c, floor_w, rng):
    """Probabilistic active-lag weights emphasizing maximum autocorrelation."""
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    if b.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    M = float(b.max())
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 1e-300:
        w = np.full(b.size, 1.0 / float(b.size), dtype=np.float64)
        return w

    beta = float(beta_c) / M
    z = beta * (b - M)
    np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0, out=z)
    w = np.exp(z)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        w.fill(1.0 / float(b.size))
    else:
        w /= s

    # Keep a small uniform mass on all lags to avoid completely strong
    # local convergence.
    u = floor_w / float(b.size)
    w = (1.0 - floor_w) * w + u

    # Occasionally probe one random lag.
    if rng is not None and rng.random() < 0.15:
        idx = int(rng.integers(0, b.size))
        w[idx] += 0.25
    s = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.full(b.size, 1.0 / float(b.size), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        w /= s
    return w


def compute_gradient_vector(p, w, L):
    """
    Gradient of the mean selected autocorrelation.

    For each selected lag k with weight w_k,
        db_k / dp_i = 2 p_{k-i}  for valid indices.
    The vector below rescales the resulting gradient to sum 2.
    """
    n = p.size
    Fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    Fw = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    C = np.fft.irfft(Fp * np.conjugate(Fw), L)
    G = 2.0 * C[:n]
    G = np.where(np.isfinite(G), G, 0.0)
    np.maximum(G, 0.0, out=G)
    s = float(G.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        G = np.full(n, 2.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        G *= (2.0 / s)
    return G


def multiplicative_update(p, G, eta_scale, sigma, rng):
    """
    Multiplicative projected step:
        q_i \propto p_i * exp(-eta_scale*n*G_i + noise)
    then normalize and add a tiny uniform floor.
    """
    n = p.size
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    logfac = -float(eta_scale) * float(n) * G
    if sigma is not None and float(sigma) > 0.0:
        logfac = logfac + float(sigma) * rng.standard_normal(n)
    if logfac.size:
        logfac = logfac - float(np.max(logfac))
    q = p * np.exp(logfac)
    q = np.where(np.isfinite(q), q, 0.0)
    np.maximum(q, 0.0, out=q)
    # Tiny floor to avoid absorbing coordinates to zero.
    floor = 1e-8
    q = (1.0 - floor) * q + (floor * (1.0 / n))
    s = float(q.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        q.fill(1.0 / n)
    else:
        q /= s
    return q


def smooth_box(p, k):
    """Mild box smoothing, renormalized."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n < 3 or k < 2:
        return norm_nonneg(p)
    k = int(min(k, n))
    ker = np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64) / float(k)
    q = np.convolve(p, ker, mode="same")
    return norm_nonneg(q)


def block_perturb(p, rng, strength=0.30):
    """Random multiplicative block perturbation."""
    p = norm_nonneg(p)
    n = p.size
    if n <= 2:
        return p
    q = p.copy()
    lo = int(max(2, n // 200))
    hi = int(max(lo + 1, n // 3))
    w = int(rng.integers(lo, hi))
    st = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - w + 1)))
    st = min(max(0, st), max(0, n - w))
    if w > 0:
        q[st:st + w] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, strength)))
    # A second smaller perturbation elsewhere.
    if n >= 2 * w and rng.random() < 0.65:
        st2 = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - w + 1)))
        st2 = min(max(0, st2), max(0, n - w))
        q[st2:st2 + w] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5 * strength)))
    return norm_nonneg(q)


def linear_pattern(n, slope=1.0, curve=0.0):
    """Smoothly increasing pattern."""
    t = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) / float(n)
    p = np.exp(float(slope) * t + float(curve) * t * t)
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-12)
    return norm_nonneg(p)


def sine_pattern(n, amp=0.8, freq=1.0, phase=0.0, drift=0.0):
    """Bounded nonnegative asymmetric oscillatory pattern."""
    t = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) / float(n)
    p = 1.0 + float(drift) * t + float(amp) * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(freq) * t + float(phase))
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-8)
    return norm_nonneg(p)


def random_pattern(n, rng):
    """Smooth random positive pattern."""
    p = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.9, n))
    p = smooth_box(p, min(7, max(3, n // 100 + 3)))
    return norm_nonneg(p)


def exact_score(seq):
    """Call the authoritative evaluator, falling back safely on error."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in seq])
        return float(val)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf


def update_best(cand, best_p, best_score):
    """Evaluate candidate exactly (with FFT fallback) and update the best."""
    cand = norm_nonneg(np.asarray(cand, dtype=np.float64))
    fs, _, _ = fast_eval(cand)
    es = exact_score(cand.tolist())
    val = es if np.isfinite(es) else fs
    if np.isfinite(val) and (not np.isfinite(best_score) or val < best_score - 1e-12):
        return cand, val
    return best_p, best_score


def optimize_sequence(p, rng, time_limit):
    """
    Multiplicative subgradient local search on nonnegative simplex.

    Returns (best_p, best_fft_score).  The best is tracked using the fast
    eval; exact evaluation is done by the caller for storage/return.
    """
    p = norm_nonneg(np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64))
    n = p.size
    if n <= 1:
        return p, fast_eval(p)[0]

    time_limit = float(max(1.0, time_limit))
    deadline = time.monotonic() + time_limit
    L = int(np.fft.next_fast_len(2 * n - 1))

    cur_p = p.copy()
    cur_score, cur_b, cur_M = fast_eval(cur_p, L)
    best_p = cur_p.copy()
    best_score = cur_score

    eta = 0.15
    beta_c = 12.0
    sigma = 0.10
    floor_w = 1e-3
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1

        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = cur_score
            best_p = cur_p.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        # Parameter annealing.
        if it % 80 == 0:
            beta_c = min(60.0, beta_c * 1.07)
            sigma = max(0.015, sigma * 0.965)

        # Strong stagnation: perturb the best sequence.
        if no_improve > 120:
            improved = False
            for attempt in range(4):
                cand = block_perturb(best_p, rng, strength=0.30 + 0.12 * attempt)
                sc2, b2, M2 = fast_eval(cand, L)
                if sc2 < best_score + 0.06:
                    cur_p = cand
                    cur_score, cur_b, cur_M = sc2, b2, M2
                    improved = True
                    break
            if not improved:
                cur_p = random_pattern(n, rng)
                cur_score, cur_b, cur_M = fast_eval(cur_p, L)

            eta = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.35))
            beta_c = float(rng.uniform(6.0, 45.0))
            sigma = float(rng.uniform(0.02, 0.22))
            no_improve = 0
            continue

        # If current has drifted badly above best, pull it back.
        if no_improve > 45 and cur_score > best_score + 0.03:
            cand = block_perturb(best_p, rng, strength=0.20)
            sc2, b2, M2 = fast_eval(cand, L)
            if sc2 < cur_score:
                cur_p, cur_score, cur_b, cur_M = cand, sc2, b2, M2
                no_improve = max(0, no_improve - 25)

        # Build active-weight vector and gradient.
        w = make_weights(cur_b, beta_c, floor_w, rng)
        G = compute_gradient_vector(cur_p, w, L)

        # Occasional inverse-gradient equalization, accepted only if helpful.
        if it % 120 == 0:
            Gsafe = np.maximum(G, 1e-12)
            eq = cur_p / Gsafe
            eq = norm_nonneg(eq)
            sc2, b2, M2 = fast_eval(eq, L)
            if sc2 < best_score - 1e-12:
                cur_p, cur_score, cur_b, cur_M = eq, sc2, b2, M2
                best_p, best_score = cur_p.copy(), sc2
                no_improve = 0
                continue

        # Line-search-like trial steps.
        if no_improve <= 25:
            factors = [1.0]
        elif no_improve <= 70:
            factors = [1.0, 0.55, 0.30]
        else:
            factors = [1.0, 0.55, 0.30, 0.12]

        accepted = False
        for f in factors:
            p_try = multiplicative_update(cur_p, G, eta * f, sigma if no_improve <= 100 else 0.03, rng)
            sc_try, b_try, M_try = fast_eval(p_try, L)

            accept = (sc_try < cur_score - 1e-13)
            # Small simulated-annealing acceptance to escape flat basins.
            if (not accept) and rng.random() < 0.04 and sc_try < cur_score + 0.012:
                accept = True

            if accept:
                cur_p, cur_score, cur_b, cur_M = p_try, sc_try, b_try, M_try
                accepted = True
                break

        if accepted:
            if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_p, best_score = cur_p.copy(), cur_score
                no_improve = 0
                eta = min(0.65, eta * 1.04)
            else:
                no_improve += 1
        else:
            # Conservative deterministic step.
            p_try = multiplicative_update(cur_p, G, eta * 0.08, 0.0, rng)
            sc_try, b_try, M_try = fast_eval(p_try, L)
            if sc_try < cur_score - 1e-13:
                cur_p, cur_score, cur_b, cur_M = p_try, sc_try, b_try, M_try
                if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best_p, best_score = cur_p.copy(), cur_score
                    no_improve = 0
            else:
                eta = max(0.02, eta * 0.78)
                no_improve += 5

    return best_p, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=0, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """Main search entry point.  Returns a valid Python list."""
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    except Exception:
        budget = 1800.0
    deadline = t0 + max(5.0, budget - 18.0)

    try:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    except Exception:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(1234567)

    # Load provided construction if available and usable.
    base_in = None
    try:
        base_in = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        base_in = base_in.reshape(-1)
    except Exception:
        base_in = None

    if base_in is None or base_in.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(base_in)):
        base_in = np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64)

    base_in = norm_nonneg(base_in)
    base_len = int(base_in.size)

    # Choose the primary length.  If the provided construction has a
    # reasonable length, keep it.  Otherwise choose a practical medium length.
    if 128 <= base_len <= 8192:
        N_target = base_len
    else:
        N_target = int(min(max(base_len, 512), 8192))

    best_p = resize_sequence(base_in, N_target)
    best_p, best_score = update_best(best_p, None, np.inf)

    # Guaranteed-ish asymmetric fallbacks: length-2 and length-3 patterns
    # have known scores below 2 after block expansion.
    fallback2 = np.array([1.0, 2.0], dtype=np.float64) / 3.0
    fallback3 = np.array([3.0, 4.0, 8.0], dtype=np.float64) / 15.0
    for fb in (fallback2, fallback3):
        cand = resize_sequence(fb, N_target)
        best_p, best_score = update_best(cand, best_p, best_score)

    print(f"[init] N_target={N_target} best_score={best_score:.8f} elapsed={time.monotonic()-t0:.2f}s")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Short-sequence discovery: good low-dimensional shapes can be
    # upsampled to longer target length and then polished.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    small_total = float(min(0.22 * budget, max(90.0, 0.12 * budget)))
    small_deadline = t0 + small_total
    if small_deadline > deadline - max(60.0, 0.25 * budget):
        small_deadline = deadline - max(60.0, 0.25 * budget)

    small_lengths = []
    if N_target <= 512:
        small_lengths = [64, max(64, N_target // 2)]
    else:
        small_lengths = [64, 128, 256]
    small_lengths = list(dict.fromkeys(int(x) for x in small_lengths if x >= 16))

    small_runs = []
    for n in small_lengths:
        small_runs.append((n, 0))
        small_runs.append((n, 1))

    best_small = None
    best_small_score = np.inf

    total_small = max(1, len(small_runs))
    for idx, (n, kind) in enumerate(small_runs):
        now = time.monotonic()
        remaining = small_deadline - now - 2.0
        if remaining < 12.0:
            break
        # Leave enough time for later large-scale runs.
        hard_stop = deadline - max(90.0, 0.30 * budget)
        if now >= hard_stop:
            break

        if kind == 0:
            init = resize_sequence(best_p, n)
        elif kind == 1:
            init = linear_pattern(n, slope=1.7, curve=0.8)
        else:
            init = random_pattern(n, rng)

        time_limit = max(9.0, min(remaining * 0.95, (hard_stop - now) * 0.92))
        p, sc = optimize_sequence(init, rng, time_limit)

        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_small_score:
            best_small = p
            best_small_score = sc

        best_p, best_score = update_best(p, best_p, best_score)

        if (time.monotonic() - t0) > (budget * 0.30) and small_deadline < deadline:
            break

    if best_small is not None:
        print(f"[small] best_short_fft={best_small_score:.8f} global={best_score:.8f} elapsed={time.monotonic()-t0:.2f}s")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Primary search at target length.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    large_reserved = 25.0
    large_deadline = deadline - large_reserved
    large_time_total = max(60.0, large_deadline - time.monotonic() - 5.0)

    large_inits = []
    if best_small is not None:
        large_inits.append(("small", resize_sequence(best_small, N_target), 2.0))
    # Current global best may be shorter; resize dynamically below.
    large_inits.append(("current", None, 2.0))
    large_inits.append(("linear", linear_pattern(N_target, 1.1, 0.0), 1.0))
    large_inits.append(("linear2", linear_pattern(N_target, 2.3, 1.4), 1.0))
    large_inits.append(("rand", random_pattern(N_target, rng), 1.0))
    large_inits.append(("sine", sine_pattern(N_target, amp=0.95, freq=1.0, phase=-0.9, drift=0.8), 1.0))

    total_w = sum(float(w) for _, _, w in large_inits)
    for name, init, w in large_inits:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now > large_deadline - 30.0:
            break
        frac = float(max(1e-6, w)) / max(1e-6, total_w)
        time_limit = (large_deadline - now - 15.0) * frac * 0.98
        if time_limit < 10.0:
            continue

        if name == "current":
            ini = resize_sequence(best_p, N_target)
        else:
            ini = init

        p, sc = optimize_sequence(ini, rng, time_limit)
        best_p, best_score = update_best(p, best_p, best_score)
        print(f"[large:{name}] fft={sc:.8f} global={best_score:.8f} "
              f"elapsed={time.monotonic()-t0:.2f}s remaining_budget={budget-(time.monotonic()-t0):.2f}s")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Last chance: polish the best known fallback/base, then re-check.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    now = time.monotonic()
    if now < deadline - 90.0:
        cand_best = resize_sequence(best_p, N_target)
        p, sc = optimize_sequence(cand_best, rng, min(300.0, deadline - now - 30.0))
        best_p, best_score = update_best(p, best_p, best_score)

    # Final deterministic fallbacks, especially if something failed.
    for fb in (fallback2, fallback3):
        if now > deadline - 5.0:
            break
        cand = resize_sequence(fb, N_target)
        best_p, best_score = update_best(cand, best_p, best_score)

    # Verify the final sequence one last time.
    out = norm_nonneg(np.asarray(best_p, dtype=np.float64))
    out, final_score = update_best(out, best_p, best_score)

    # Build the final Python list.  If exact evaluator is unavailable and
    # fast estimate is the only score, this list is still valid and safe.
    out = np.asarray(out, dtype=np.float64)
    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.01:
        # Evaluator requires sum > 0.01.  Use a safe fallback normalized to 1.
        out = linear_pattern(max(64, N_target), 1.0, 0.0)
        s = 1.0

    out_list = [float(x) for x in out.tolist()]
    # Ensure the list is nonempty and all entries are valid finite numbers.
    if not out_list:
        out_list = [1.0]
    else:
        bad = any((not np.isfinite(x)) or x < 0.0 for x in out_list)
        if bad:
            out_list = [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) if np.isfinite(x) else 0.0 for x in out_list]
        ss = float(sum(out_list))
        if ss < 0.01:
            out_list = [1.0 / float(len(out_list))] * len(out_list)

    print(f"[final] score={final_score if np.isfinite(final_score) else 'n/a'} len={len(out_list)} elapsed={time.monotonic()-t0:.2f}s")
    return out_list
```
